## Engine objects

Engine objects (actors, heroes, players, armies, ...) appear in Lua as references. A reference points at the
live object: reading a property always returns its current value.

```lua
local hero = h7.first("H7AdventureHero")

h7.log(hero)                     -- H7AdventureHero sce_sephinroth.TheWorld.PersistentLevel.H7AdventureHero_0
local player = hero:GetPlayer()  -- call an UnrealScript function
h7.log(player:GetName())
```

**Properties** are read and written by name, exactly as declared in UnrealScript:

```lua
local stack = h7.first("H7CreatureStack")
h7.log(stack.mHumanDamageMultiplier)
stack.mHumanDamageMultiplier = 2
```

Writing a property that doesn't exist is an error; reading one returns `nil`.

**Functions** are called with `:` and take their parameters in declaration order:

```lua
local resources = player:GetResourceSet()
resources:ModifyCurrency(500, true)     -- ModifyCurrency(int modAmount, bool updateGui)
```

- The function's return value comes back first, followed by the values of its `out` parameters, in order.
  Struct and array results come back as plain Lua tables.
- Missing arguments (or `nil`) are passed as zero: `0`, `false`, `""`, no object. UnrealScript default values
  are **not** applied, so pass optional parameters explicitly when their default isn't zero.
- If a property and a function share a name, call the function with `obj:call("Name", ...)`.
- Calls run the function exactly as the game would, including any hooks on it.

**Helper methods** on every object (lowercase, so they never clash with game members):

| Method | Returns |
|---|---|
| `get_name()` | Object name, e.g. `H7AdventureHero_0` |
| `get_full_name()` | Class and path, e.g. `H7AdventureHero sce_sephinroth.TheWorld.PersistentLevel.H7AdventureHero_0` (also what `tostring` gives) |
| `get_path_name()` | Path without the class |
| `get_class()` | The object's class (an object) |
| `get_outer()` | The object that contains it |
| `get_default()` | On a class: its default object (`Default__ClassName`) |
| `get_super()` | On a class, struct or function: its parent |
| `get_address()` | Memory address as an integer (useful as a table key) |
| `is_valid()` | `false` once the game has destroyed the object |
| `is_a(class)` | Whether the object is of `class` or a subclass (class object or name) |
| `call(name, ...)` | Calls a function by name |

Two references to the same object are equal (`==`). An object can be destroyed while your mod still holds a
reference (for example when a map unloads); using it then raises *"engine object ... is no longer valid"*. Don't
keep object references across maps, or check `is_valid()` first.

## Values and types

| UnrealScript | Lua |
|---|---|
| `int`, `byte`, enums | integer (enums are their numeric value; see `h7.enum`) |
| `float` | number |
| `bool` | boolean |
| `string` | string |
| `name` | string (a name the engine doesn't know yet is added to its name table) |
| object, class, interface | object reference, or `nil` for `none` |
| struct | live struct reference: `v.X` reads and `v.X = 1` writes the field in place |
| `array<T>` | live array reference (see below) |
| fixed array (`int X[4]`) | a Lua table copy; assign a table to write it |
| delegate | `{object = ..., func = "Name"}`; pass the same shape to a delegate parameter, e.g. `widget:AddEventListener("CLIK_click", {object = cntl, func = "Options"})` |

Assigning an object to an object property checks its type. A struct property can be assigned a struct
reference of the same type or a Lua table of fields: `obj.Location = {X = 0, Y = 0, Z = 100}`.

**Arrays** are live views of the engine array:

```lua
local obelisks = player.mVisitedObelisks   -- array<H7Obelisk> property of H7Player
h7.log(#obelisks)                          -- length
local first = obelisks[1]                  -- 1-based, like Lua
obelisks[1] = other                        -- replace an element
obelisks:add(value)                        -- append; returns the new length
obelisks:remove(2)                         -- remove element 2, shifting the rest down
```

Assigning a Lua table (or another engine array) to an array property replaces its contents:
`obj.mNames = {"a", "b"}`. The same works for array parameters of a function call; elements are converted like
properties, so an array of structs takes a table of tables:

```lua
-- GFxObject.Invoke(string Member, array<ASValue> args)
local AS = h7.enum("ASType")
widget:Invoke("setTextColor", { { Type = AS.AS_Number, n = 0x9be37a } })
```

Leaving an array argument out passes an empty array.

An array *returned by a function* is a plain Lua table copy instead (`player:GetHeroes()`), since the engine's
copy is gone once the call returns. UnrealScript's `private`/`protected` don't apply: mods can read and write
every property.

`h7.to_table(value)` copies a struct, array or object's properties into a plain Lua table: handy for logging or
for keeping a snapshot.

## Looking things up

| Function | Returns |
|---|---|
| `h7.first(class)` | The first live instance of `class` (or a subclass), or `nil` |
| `h7.find_all(class [, include_templates])` | All live instances. Class defaults and archetypes (templates stored in content packages) are skipped unless `include_templates` is `true` |
| `h7.find(path)` | The object with this path: `"MMH7Game.H7Calendar"`, or with its class to be exact: `"Class MMH7Game.H7Calendar"`, `"Function MMH7Game.H7Calendar.NextDay"` |
| `h7.find_class(name)` | A class by `"Package.Class"` or bare `"Class"` name |
| `h7.enum(name)` | A table of an enum's values: `h7.enum("EMyEnum").SOME_VALUE` → integer |
| `h7.properties(x)` / `h7.functions(x)` | Lists of `{name, type, owner, offset}` / `{name, type, owner, flags}`, including inherited members (`type` is the engine type, e.g. `IntProperty`; `owner` the declaring class). `x` is a class or struct name, an object, or a struct reference |
| `h7.signature(func)` | The function's declaration as text, e.g. `function string Engine.Controller.GetHumanReadableName()` |

`class` can be a name (`"H7AdventureHero"` or `"MMH7Game.H7AdventureHero"`) or a class object. `find_all` scans
every engine object, so call it when you need it rather than every frame.

## Creating objects

Mods can create new engine objects, most usefully **copies of game data** (archetypes: the templates the game
spawns creatures, heroes, towns and buildings from). A copy starts with every property of its source; change
what you need and add it to the game's lists. This is how a mod adds a new creature, hero or a whole faction.

```lua
local pkg = h7.package("MyMod")                 -- a package of your own: gives copies stable names
local src = h7.find("Creature_Pixie.A_CreaturePixie")
local sprite, created = h7.clone(src, "A_CreatureFrostSprite", pkg, h7.flags.RootSet)
sprite.mNameInst = "Frost Sprite"               -- name shown in game (see below)
sprite.mHitpoints = 20
```

| Function | |
|---|---|
| `h7.clone(source, name [, outer [, flags]])` | A copy of `source` (same class, every property copied) called `name` inside `outer` (default: the transient package). Returns the object and `true`; if `outer` already holds an object called `name` of the same class it is returned with `false` instead, so the same code can run again after a hot reload. Archetypes stay archetypes. `flags` adds object flags |
| `h7.new_object(class, {name=, outer=, template=, flags=, archetype=, replace=})` | A new object of `class`, with properties from `template` (or the class defaults). Fails if `outer` already has an object called `name`, unless `replace = true` |
| `h7.package(name)` | The top-level package `name`, created if it doesn't exist |
| `h7.name(str)` | Adds `str` to the engine's name table and returns it |
| `h7.can_create()` | `false` if the engine couldn't verify the game functions it needs (see the log); the functions above then fail |
| `h7.flags` | Object flags: `Public`, `Standalone`, `Transient`, `Archetype`, `RootSet` |

Things to know:

- **Keep copies alive.** Outside the editor the garbage collector frees anything nothing references, on every map
  change. Pass `h7.flags.RootSet` (never freed) or make sure game data the game keeps references the copy.
- **Lazily loaded references.** Many Heroes VII references (creature and hero models `mVisuals`, icons, town
  screens, `H7Faction.mStartTown`...) are `DynLoadObjectProperty` fields that the game loads from the object's
  package when first needed and releases again with `Del*` functions. A copy has no package, so it can never
  load them: call `source:DynLoadObjectProperty("mVisuals")` (or `DynLoadObjectArrayProperty` for arrays) on the
  source **before** cloning, and stop the game's `Del*` functions for your copies (and their sources) with a
  pre-hook. A creature without its model crashes the game when a battle starts.
- **Names and texts** are localised by object name, which a copy doesn't share with its source: the game shows
  the copy's fallback text (usually English). Set the cached texts yourself, e.g. `mNameInst` on units, or
  hook the `GetName` of the class.
- **Save games** store references by object path (`MyMod.A_CreatureFrostSprite`), so create your copies with
  the same names before a save is loaded (at the main menu), and never rename them.

## Hooks

A hook runs your Lua function whenever a game function is called.

```lua
-- Before the function runs. Return true to block it.
local id = h7.hook("Package.Class.Function", function(self, args)
end)

-- After the function has run. Return a value to replace its return value.
local id = h7.hook_post("Package.Class.Function", function(self, args, result)
end)

h7.unhook(id)
```

`h7.hook(func, fn, "post")` is the same as `h7.hook_post(func, fn)`. The function is named
`Package.Class.Function` or passed as a function object.

**Parameters.**
- `self` is the object the function was called on.
- `args` gives access to the function's parameters by name: `args.Result`, `args.resultIdx`. You can also assign
  them (`args.Amount = 10` in a pre-hook changes what the function receives). `out` parameters can be read and
  written the same way.
- In a post-hook, `args` shows the values after the function ran, and `result` is its return value (absent for
  functions that return nothing).
- `args` and any struct or array read from it are only valid while your hook runs. Copy what you need
  (`h7.to_table`) if you want to keep it.

**Blocking.** A pre-hook that returns `true` stops the original function from running. The caller then gets a
zero return value (`0`, `false`, `""`, `nil`). Post-hooks still run.

**Changing the result.** A post-hook that returns a non-`nil` value replaces the function's return value.
Returning nothing leaves it unchanged.

```lua
-- CombatXP: 50% more experience from combat
h7.hook_post("MMH7Game.H7CombatArmy.GetKilledCreaturesXP", function(army, args, xp)
    if xp > 0 then return math.floor(xp * 1.5 + 0.5) end
end)

-- DamageScale: read an argument and call methods on it
h7.hook_post("MMH7Game.H7GameProcessor.CalculateDamage", function(processor, args)
    local result, idx = args.Result, args.resultIdx
    if not result or result:IsHeal(idx) then return end
    result:SetDamage(math.floor(result:GetDamage(idx) * 1.25 + 0.5), idx)
end)
```

**What a hook sees.**
- Hooks on **UnrealScript functions** see every call, whether it comes from other UnrealScript or from the
  engine's C++ code.
- A hook applies to the function exactly as named. If a subclass overrides the function, calls on subclass
  objects run the override, so hook the override too (`H7CreatureStack.ApplyDamage` is separate from
  `H7Unit.ApplyDamage`).
- **Native functions** (implemented in C++, `native` in `h7.signature`) can be hooked, but `args` is `nil`
  and they can't be blocked.
- Latent and iterator functions (`Sleep`, `foreach` iterators) only report calls made from C++.

**Several hooks** on one function (from one or more mods) all run, in the order they were added.

**Speed.** Some functions run hundreds of times a second (for example during combat). Keep hooks on them cheap:
check the cheapest condition first, and cache answers per object, as the HeroMovement example does:

```lua
local is_local = {}
h7.on("map_loaded", function() is_local = {} end)

h7.hook_post("MMH7Game.H7EditorHero.GetMovementPoints", function(hero, args, points)
    local key = hero:get_address()
    if is_local[key] == nil then
        local player = hero:GetPlayer()
        is_local[key] = player ~= nil and player:IsControlledByLocalPlayer()
    end
    if is_local[key] then return math.floor(points * 1.5 + 0.5) end
end)
```

## Events and timers

```lua
local id = h7.on("map_loaded", function(map) ... end)
h7.off(id)
```

| Event | Arguments | When |
|---|---|---|
| `tick` | `dt` (seconds since the last frame) | Every frame |
| `map_loaded` | map name (lowercase), e.g. `h7logoentry`, `councilhub` (main menu), `sce_sephinroth` | When a different map is active. Battles don't trigger it |
| `mods_loaded` | number of mods | After all mods have loaded (also after `h7.reload()`) |
| `mod_toggled` | mod name, `enabled` | After a mod was loaded or unloaded by `h7.set_mod_enabled` (the Mods menu) |
| `setting_changed` | mod name, key, new value | After a mod setting changed (Mods menu, `mod.settings`, `h7.set_mod_setting`) |

Timers run on game time:

```lua
local id = h7.after(5, function() h7.log("5 seconds later") end)   -- once
local id = h7.every(60, function() h7.log("every minute") end)     -- repeating
h7.cancel(id)
```

Events, timers and the hot-reload check run on the game's frame. **They pause while the game window is in the
background**, because the game stops updating then.

## Saving data

```lua
local data = mod.load()            -- {} the first time
data.launches = (data.launches or 0) + 1
mod.save(data)
```

`mod.save(table)` writes `<Game>\H7ModEngine\data\<ModName>.lua`; `mod.load()` reads it back. Numbers,
strings, booleans and nested tables are saved; anything else (functions, engine objects) is dropped. The data
belongs to the mod, not to a savegame.

## Logging and debugging

| Function | Writes to `logs\engine.log` |
|---|---|
| `h7.log(...)`, `print(...)` | `[MyMod] message` |
| `h7.warn(...)` | `WARNING [MyMod] message` |
| `h7.error(...)` | `ERROR [MyMod] message` |

Hooks, event handlers and timers run as the mod that registered them: their log lines carry its name, and any
hook, handler or timer they create belongs to that mod too (and is removed when it unloads).

Errors inside hooks, event handlers and timers are caught and logged as `ERROR in hook ... (MyMod)` with a stack
trace; the game keeps running and the hook stays active.

**The exec folder** runs Lua in the running game. Copy a `.lua` file into `<Game>\H7ModEngine\exec\`. Within
about a second it runs, its output and return values go to the log (`exec: => ...`), and the file is renamed to
`.done`. Exec scripts share the global environment, not a mod's.

```lua
-- exec\check.lua
local hero = h7.first("H7AdventureHero")
return hero, hero:GetMovementPoints()
```

`h7.exec("command")` runs a console command through the local player controller and returns its output.

## Configuration

`<Game>\H7ModEngine\H7ModEngine.ini`:

```ini
[Mods]
; Comma-separated mod names, written by the in-game Mods menu. They override the manifests' enabled field.
Enabled=DailyGold
Disabled=HelloWorld

; Mod settings, written by the Mods menu (one section per mod; see Settings)
[Mod.DamageScale]
multiplier=1.5

[Dev]
; Write names.txt / objects.txt to dumps\ at startup (default 1)
DumpSDK=1
; Engine development only (default 0): raw layout snapshots used for engine calibration
DumpProbe=0
; Engine development only (default 0): the unpacked game executable (~100 MB)
DumpImage=0
```

## Reference

The complete reference, with every parameter, return value and error, is in the
[API reference](https://whaileee.github.io/H7ModEngine/reference/) (in this repository: `docs/reference/`). A summary:

**h7**

| | |
|---|---|
| `h7.version` | Engine version string |
| `h7.log/warn/error(...)` | [Logging](#logging-and-debugging) |
| `h7.first`, `h7.find_all`, `h7.find`, `h7.find_class`, `h7.enum`, `h7.properties`, `h7.functions`, `h7.signature` | [Looking things up](#looking-things-up) |
| `h7.to_table(value)` | Plain Lua copy of a struct, array or object |
| `h7.clone`, `h7.new_object`, `h7.package`, `h7.name`, `h7.can_create`, `h7.flags` | [Creating objects](#creating-objects) |
| `h7.hook(func, fn [, "pre" \| "post"])`, `h7.hook_post(func, fn)`, `h7.unhook(id)` | [Hooks](#hooks) |
| `h7.on(event, fn)`, `h7.off(id)` | [Events](#events-and-timers) |
| `h7.after(seconds, fn)`, `h7.every(seconds, fn)`, `h7.cancel(id)` | [Timers](#events-and-timers) |
| `h7.mods()` | `{name, version, author, description, category, enabled, loaded, hidden, settings}` for every installed mod (`settings`: how many it declares) (`enabled` but not `loaded`: it failed to load, see the log) |
| `h7.set_mod_enabled(name, on)` | Turn a mod on or off; saved in the ini, applied on the next frame |
| `h7.mod_settings(name)`, `h7.set_mod_setting(name, key, value)` | A mod's settings with their values; change one (see Settings) |
| `h7.reload()` | Unload and reload all mods (on the next frame) |
| `h7.exec(command)` | Run a console command |

**Per mod**: `mod.name`, `mod.version`, `mod.author`, `mod.dir`, `mod.require(name)`, `mod.save(table)`,
`mod.load()`, and an optional global `function on_unload()`.

**Example mods** (in `mods\`; all except HelloWorld ship disabled; turn them on in the Mods menu):

| Mod | Shows |
|---|---|
| ModManager | The main menu's Mods entry, the mod list and the settings controls: adding Flash clips to a game window and reading mouse input |
| HelloWorld | Events, timers, saved data, `on_unload` |
| DailyGold | Post-hook on a game function; finding objects; calling functions |
| CombatXP | Replacing a return value |
| DamageScale | Reading hook arguments (including an `out` parameter) and calling methods on them |
| HeroMovement | Filtering by owner; caching per object in a hot hook |

## Limitations

- Built for Heroes VII **v2.2.1** (64-bit). Other versions may not work.
- Mods change the local game only. **Multiplayer is untested** and will likely desync when a mod changes
  gameplay.
- Native functions: hooks get no arguments and can't block.
- New mod folders need `h7.reload()` or a restart.
- Flash objects (`GFxObject`) returned by `GetObject`/`AttachMovie` are freed by the engine's garbage
  collector when no UnrealScript object holds them. Keep the clip's name and call `GetObject` again when you
  need it, instead of storing the object.
- `GetObject` only reaches display objects (named clips, buttons, labels) and objects created from Unreal
  (`CreateObject`/`CreateArray`); members holding arrays or objects created by the movie's own ActionScript
  return `nil`.
- The game's Flash menus cache every translation they ask for (`H7FlashMovieCntl.FlashLocalize`) for the rest of
  the session, so changing a translation through a hook also changes it everywhere else that key is used.
- Mods run on the game thread: a slow hook slows the game down.
- In the Mods menu the selection goes back to the first row after a mod is enabled or disabled (the list is
  rebuilt; its rows can't be selected from Lua).

```

`docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`:

```md
# H7ModEngine development guide

This guide is for people working on the engine itself: the C++ DLL, the Lua runtime and bindings, and the
bundled mods. If you want to write mods, read [API.md](API.md) instead.

- [Setup](#setup)
- [Building and deploying](#building-and-deploying)
- [The development loop](#the-development-loop)
- [Architecture](#architecture)
- [Source tour](#source-tour)
- [Hooks in detail](#hooks-in-detail)
- [The Lua side](#the-lua-side)
- [Testing in game](#testing-in-game)
- [Finding things in the game](#finding-things-in-the-game)
- [Reverse-engineering notes](#reverse-engineering-notes)
- [Working with the game's Flash UI](#working-with-the-games-flash-ui)
- [Extending the API](#extending-the-api)
- [Conventions](#conventions)
- [CI](#ci)
- [Troubleshooting](#troubleshooting)

## Setup

**Required**

- Windows 10/11 x64.
- Visual Studio 2022 or the Build Tools for Visual Studio, with the **C++ x64 build tools** workload component.
  `build.bat` finds any edition with `vswhere`.
- Git. The repository uses a submodule for Lua, so clone with `--recursive`, or run
  `git submodule update --init` after a normal clone.
- Might & Magic Heroes VII **v2.2.1** (Ubisoft Connect, 64-bit). The engine is only tested against this build.

**Useful**

- Git Bash: `tests/run_ingame.sh` is a bash script.
- Python 3: the scripts in `re/`.
- `cdb.exe` (Debugging Tools for Windows, part of the Windows SDK): static disassembly of the unpacked game image
  and attaching to the running game.
- The decompiled UnrealScript sources of the game (`<Package>\Classes\*.uc`, e.g. from the H7 SDK / UDK export).
  They are the fastest way to find the function you want to hook; see
  [Finding things in the game](#finding-things-in-the-game).

```bash
git clone --recursive https://github.com/Whaileee/H7ModEngine.git
```

## Building and deploying

```bash
build.bat
```

builds `bin\dinput8.dll` (and `bin\dinput8.pdb`). It compiles Lua 5.4.7 from `third_party\lua` **as C++**
(`/TP /EHsc`), so a Lua error raised inside engine code unwinds as a C++ exception through the C++ frames
instead of `longjmp`-ing over their destructors. The engine is C++20, `/MT`, linked with `src\proxy\dinput8.def`.

```bash
build.bat deploy
```

builds and then installs into the game folder:

| Source | Destination | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `bin\dinput8.dll` | `<Game>\Binaries\Win64\dinput8.dll` | replaced every time |
| `dist\H7ModEngine.ini` | `<Game>\H7ModEngine\H7ModEngine.ini` | only if there is none yet: your local settings survive |
| `dist\mods\ModManager` | `<Game>\H7ModEngine\mods\ModManager` | overwritten: edits you made in the game copy are lost. `build.bat deploy examples` also installs the example mods |
| `dist\lib\*` | `<Game>\H7ModEngine\lib\` | shared Lua libraries, if any |

The game folder defaults to `N:\Ubisoft\games\Might and Magic Heroes VII`; set `H7_GAME_DIR` to yours:

```bash
set H7_GAME_DIR=C:\Games\Might and Magic Heroes VII
```

The DLL can't be replaced while the game runs (Windows keeps it loaded). Lua files can: see the next section.

## The development loop

1. `build.bat deploy` with the game closed.
2. Start the game through Ubisoft Connect. Starting `MMH7Game-Win64-Shipping.exe` directly makes it exit and
   relaunch through Connect, so a short-lived first process is normal; tools that wait for the game must not
   treat that first exit as the end of the run.
3. Follow the log: `<Game>\H7ModEngine\logs\engine.log` (the previous run is in `engine.prev.log`).

   ```bash
   tail -f "/n/Ubisoft/games/Might and Magic Heroes VII/H7ModEngine/logs/engine.log"
   ```

   A healthy start looks like this:

   ```
   H7ModEngine starting; game image 0000000140000000-0000000146B94000
   Script loaded: 27743 names, 12792 objects
   Calibrated: Struct{Super=78 Children=80 Size=88} Prop{...} Func{...} Class{CDO=1d4} Enum{Names=68}
   Script functions: 18472, bound to ProcessInternal(game+266de40): 18471, unbound: 1, other: 0
   Tick hook on Function Engine.GameViewportClient.PostRender: ok
   Init complete; Lua starts on the next frame
   Lua 5.4 ready (H7ModEngine 0.1.0)
   Loaded mod HelloWorld 1.0 by H7ModEngine
   Map: h7logoentry
   Map: councilhub
   ```

4. Change Lua without restarting:
   - **Mods hot-reload.** Edit the game copy in `<Game>\H7ModEngine\mods\<Mod>\`; within a second the engine
     unloads the mod (removing its hooks, handlers and timers) and loads it again. Copy the change back to
     `dist\mods\` when it works, or edit in `dist\` and copy over; `deploy` overwrites the game copies.
   - **The exec folder** runs a script once in the running game: drop `something.lua` into
     `<Game>\H7ModEngine\exec\`. It runs on the next check (about once a second), its return values are logged
     as `exec: => ...`, and the file is renamed to `.done` (even when it fails, and overwriting an older
     `.done` of the same name, so keep the source in `tests\` and copy it in). Exec scripts use the global
     environment, not a mod's; `_G` is shared with mods, which is handy for poking at a mod's state.
5. Only C++ changes need a restart.

The game pauses its main loop while its window is in the background: ticks, timers, hot reload and the exec
folder stop until it is focused again. While it runs but is covered by other windows it keeps ticking but stops
drawing.

## Architecture

```
MMH7Game-Win64-Shipping.exe
  └─ loads dinput8.dll (ours, from Binaries\Win64)
       ├─ proxy: forwards DirectInput exports to the system dinput8.dll
       └─ InitThread
            1. wait for GNames / GObjects (the exe is packed and unpacks itself first)
            2. wait for script packages (Engine.Actor.Touch, MMH7Game.H7Creature exist)
            3. Calibrate(): learn UObject/UStruct/UProperty/UFunction/UClass layouts from known objects
            4. wait for the GameViewportClient, find UObject::ProcessInternal
            5. hook Engine.GameViewportClient.PostRender  ──► OnGameTick() every frame, on the game thread
                                                                ├─ first tick: create the Lua state, load mods
                                                                ├─ timers, tick event, mod toggles
                                                                └─ once a second: hot reload, map change, exec
```

Everything after step 5 runs on the game thread, inside the engine's own frame. Lua is single-threaded and never
touched from the init thread.

**No code patching.** The executable is protected and packed, so the engine never writes to its code. It only
changes heap data the engine itself reads: `UFunction::Func` pointers, `UFunction::Script` bytecode arrays, and a
few engine objects it creates (a cloned native `UFunction`). This is why hooks are built from the engine's own
mechanisms (see [Hooks in detail](#hooks-in-detail)).

**Discovery instead of offsets.** Global tables are found by scanning the module for structures that look like
`TArray<FNameEntry*>` and `TArray<UObject*>` and checking them against known names. Field offsets are then
measured on objects whose values are known (for example, the size and parameter count of `Engine.Actor.Touch`).
The results are logged in the `Calibrated:` line. When calibration fails, the engine writes `dumps\probe.txt`
and stays out of the way.

### Confirmed layout (v2.2.1, x64)

Engine structs are **packed to 4 bytes**: pointers are not always 8-aligned (for example `UStruct::Script` is at
0x8C). Scan with a step of 4.

| Type | Field | Offset |
|---|---|---|
| UObject | VfTable / ObjectFlags / InternalIndex / Outer / Name / Class / Archetype | 0x00 / 0x10 / 0x38 / 0x40 / 0x48 / 0x50 / 0x58 (size 0x60) |
| UField | Next | 0x60 |
| UStruct | SuperField / Children / PropertySize / Script | 0x78 / 0x80 / 0x88 / 0x8C (size 0xD0) |
| UFunction | FunctionFlags / iNative / FriendlyName / NumParms / ParmsSize / ReturnValueOffset / Func | 0xD0 / 0xD4 / 0xD8 / 0xE1 / 0xE2 / 0xE4 / 0xF0 (size 0xF8) |
| UProperty | ArrayDim / ElementSize / PropertyFlags / Offset | 0x68 / 0x6C / 0x70 / 0x8C (size 0xA8) |
| UBoolProperty | BitMask | 0xA8 |
| UEnum | Names (`TArray<FName>`) | 0x68 |
| UClass | ClassDefaultObject | 0x1D4 (calibrated) |
| FFrame | Node / Object / Code / Locals / OutParms | +0x14 / +0x1C / +0x24 / +0x2C / +0x3C |

Other facts: `ObjectFlags` 0x200 = class default object, 0x400 = archetype. `ProcessEvent` is `vtable[70]`,
found at runtime by watching who calls a hooked event. The engine allocator is tbbmalloc: strings and arrays the
engine may free later are allocated with `scalable_realloc` (`ue3::EngineRealloc`).

## Source tour

| Path | What it does |
|---|---|
| `src/proxy/` | `dinput8.def` and `proxy.cpp`: export forwarding to the system DLL |
| `src/engine/dllmain.cpp` | `DllMain` and the init thread (the sequence above); finds the viewport and installs the tick hook |
| `src/engine/paths.*` | `<Game>\H7ModEngine\` paths and `H7ModEngine.ini` access |
| `src/core/log.*` | The engine log (shared-write, since two game processes overlap briefly at launch) |
| `src/core/memory.*` | SEH-guarded reads/writes (`mem::Read`, `mem::ReadOr`), module bounds, region scans, `.pdata` function starts |
| `src/ue3/core.*` | GNames/GObjects discovery, names, object iteration, `FindObject`/`FindClass` |
| `src/ue3/layout.h` | The calibrated offsets (`ue3::L`) |
| `src/ue3/reflection.*` | Calibration; fields, properties, functions, enums, `Describe(prop)`, `EngineRealloc` |
| `src/ue3/hooks.*` | `ProcessEvent` discovery and calls; script and native function hooks |
| `src/ue3/factory.*` | Creating objects: `StaticConstructObject`, `FName(TCHAR*, FNAME_Add)` and `GetTransientPackage` at fixed v2.2.1 addresses, each checked against its first bytes before use |
| `src/ue3/dumper.*` | `names.txt`, `objects.txt`, `probe.txt` and the unpacked image dump (`[Dev]` settings) |
| `src/lua/runtime.*` | Lua state, mod discovery/order/environments, hot reload, mod toggles, events, timers, saved data, exec folder |
| `src/lua/bindings.cpp` | The `h7` table and the object/struct/array proxies: marshaling between Lua and engine memory, calls, hooks |

`[Dev]` settings in `H7ModEngine.ini`: `DumpSDK=1` writes the object index modders use; `DumpProbe=1` writes
raw layout snapshots (calibration work); `DumpImage=1` writes the unpacked executable (~100 MB, for static
analysis).

## Hooks in detail

UnrealScript functions are called two ways, and a hook has to catch both:

- **C++ → script**: `ProcessEvent(obj, fn, params)` → `fn->Func` (for script functions, `UObject::ProcessInternal`).
- **Script → script**: the VM calls `ProcessInternal` directly; `UFunction::Func` is never consulted.

So swapping `Func` only works for natives and C++-invoked events. Script functions get a **bytecode trampoline**
instead (`ue3/hooks.cpp`):

- The function's `Script` array is replaced by
  `[original optional-parameter entries] 04 1C <fake native UFunction*> 16 04 0B 53`.
  `ProcessInternal` steps statements until `EX_Return` (0x04), then evaluates one expression as the return value;
  that expression is an `EX_FinalFunction` (0x1C) call to a **fake native**: a clone of `Core.Object.Sin`
  (final, native, iNative 0) whose `Func` is `ScriptHookNative`.
- `ScriptHookNative` runs the pre listeners (any can block), then runs the original body with `ProcessInternal`
  on a copy of the frame whose `Node` is a shadow `UFunction` holding the original `Script` and whose `Code`
  points just past the optional-parameter entries, then runs the post listeners (which can replace the return
  value).
- **Optional parameters**: the engine steps one entry per optional parameter at the start of `Script` while
  binding the call, before `ProcessInternal` runs, so those entries must stay at the front. An entry is either
  `EX_DefaultParmValue` (`49 <u16 len> <expr> 15`) or a lone `EX_Nothing` (`0B`) for a parameter without a
  default. The parser (`DefaultParmBlocks`) accepts exactly these and otherwise falls back to a `Func` swap and
  logs it; it never guesses, because a wrong guess crashes the VM with `Unknown code token`.
- **Out parameters** of script → script calls live in the caller's frame. `FFrame::OutParms` (+0x3C) is a list
  of `{UProperty*, PropAddr, Next}`; hook `args` resolve out parameters through it (`hooks::OutParmAddr`).
- Native, latent and iterator functions use the `Func` swap. Hooks on natives see no arguments and can't block.

Several listeners can share one hooked function; they are removed with their mod. Opcode handlers are in
`GNatives` (game+0x2352220); disassemble with `cdb -z <unpacked exe> -c "u <address>; q"`.

## The Lua side

**Proxies.** Engine objects are full userdata (`ObjRef`) holding the `UObject*`; `__index` looks the name up as a
property or function of the object's class (cached per class). Structs (`StructRef`) and dynamic arrays
(`ArrayRef`) are live views into engine memory. Values that point into a hook's parameters carry a frame id and
become invalid when the hook returns. Function return values that are structs or arrays are copied to Lua
tables, because the parameter buffer is gone after the call.

**Marshaling** goes through `PushElem` (engine → Lua) and `WriteElem` (Lua → engine), keyed by property kind:
numbers, bools (bitmask), names (must already exist in the name table), strings (`FString` via the engine
allocator), objects (type-checked), structs (another struct or a table of fields), arrays (a table replaces the
contents), delegates (`{object=, func=}`).

**Calls** (`CallUFunction`): parameters are written into a zeroed buffer of `ParmsSize`, missing arguments stay
zero (not UnrealScript defaults), the call goes through `ProcessEvent`, then the return value and out
parameters are pushed, and strings and arrays left in the buffer are freed.

**Mods** (`runtime.cpp`): every folder in `mods\` is discovered; enabled ones load alphabetically, then after
their `load_after` entries. Each mod runs `main.lua` in its own environment (globals stay in the mod, reads fall
through to the shared globals). Everything a mod registers — hooks, event handlers, timers — is tagged with the
mod's id and removed when it unloads. Callbacks run inside `ModScope(mod)`, so anything they register belongs to
the same mod, and their log lines carry its name. `h7.set_mod_enabled` writes the ini and the load or unload
happens on the next tick, never inside the caller.

**Errors** inside callbacks are caught with a traceback (`Report`) and logged; the hook or handler stays
installed.

## Testing in game

There are no offline unit tests: almost everything depends on the live engine. Tests are Lua scripts in `tests\`
that run through the exec folder and report to the log.

| Script | Run it | Checks |
|---|---|---|
| `selftest.lua` | at the main menu | lookups, reads/writes, structs, arrays (incl. writing them and array arguments), calls, hooks |
| `hooktest.lua` | at the main menu | pre-hook blocking, post-hook return override, arguments, script → script calls, unhook |
| `sigdump.lua` | any map | logs signatures of the gameplay functions the example mods use |
| `daytrace.lua` | adventure map | call-count tracer for ~117 calendar/combat functions: end a day, fight a battle, read which ran |
| `modwindow_autotest.lua` | main menu | drives the Mods window: open, enable/disable DailyGold, back, then checks the game's map selection was restored |
| `swfdump.lua` | main menu | copies every loaded Flash movie to `dumps\swf\` |
| `menulab.lua`, `modlistlab.lua` | main menu | the experiments the Mods menu was built from |

Run one by copying it into `exec\` and reading the log, e.g. a successful self-test ends with

```
[selftest] array argument         OK    first,2.0
[selftest] call function          OK    <player name>
exec: => selftest dispatched
[selftest] hook fired 154 times in 2s
```

**`tests/run_ingame.sh`** automates this and takes screenshots:

```bash
tests/run_ingame.sh tests/modwindow_autotest.lua /c/temp/shots 60
```

It copies the script into `exec\`, prints the log lines it produces, saves `<dir>\<name>.png` for every line
ending in `SHOT <name>` (via `tests/shot.ps1`, which captures the game window with `PrintWindow`, on any display)
and stops at a line ending in `DONE`. A test drives the game with `h7.after` steps, logs the state it checks,
and writes `SHOT`/`DONE` lines; see `modwindow_autotest.lua`. Screenshots only show a current frame while the
game window is visible (it stops drawing when covered), so assert on logged state and use pictures to look.

Before a pull request that touches the engine, run at least `selftest.lua` and `hooktest.lua` at the main menu,
plus a test for what you changed, and note the results in the PR.

## Finding things in the game

1. **Search the object index.** With `DumpSDK=1`, `dumps\objects.txt` lists every class, function, property and
   object loaded at startup (`Function MMH7Game.H7Calendar.NextDay`, ...), and `names.txt` every name.
2. **Read the decompiled UnrealScript.** Grep the `.uc` sources for the behaviour you want and follow callers;
   it is much faster than tracing at runtime. Prefer hooking script functions (bodies in the sources) over
   natives (`native function`), since native hooks get no arguments.
3. **Ask the engine.** `h7.signature("MMH7Game.H7Calendar.NextDay")`, `h7.functions(obj)`,
   `h7.properties("MMH7Game.H7AdventureHero")`, `obj:get_class()`; run them through the exec folder.
4. **Trace.** Copy `daytrace.lua` and adapt its function list to see which candidates actually run, and how
   often, while you play.

Known good targets are listed in `RESUME.md` (new day, damage, combat XP, movement points).

## Reverse-engineering notes

- **The executable is packed** (stub entry in `.text1`, `.xtext` decrypted in place at startup), so the file on
  disk is useless for static analysis. Set `DumpImage=1`, start the game once, and use
  `<Game>\H7ModEngine\dumps\MMH7Game_unpacked.exe`. Keep dumps out of git (`re/dump/` is ignored).
- `cdb -z <unpacked exe> -c "u <address>; q"` disassembles; addresses in the log are given as `game+<rva>`.
- `re/scripts/pe_scan.py <exe>`: sections, anchor strings and UE3 native-function tables (`execFoo` pairs).
- `re/swfinfo.py <movie.swf> [--strings]`: tags, exported symbols and AS3 classes with their instance and static
  members, for the Scaleform movies dumped by `tests/swfdump.lua`.
- A Ghidra project may live in `re/ghidra_proj/` (ignored).
- `RESUME.md` is the running notebook: what was tried, what was verified in which in-game run, and why.

## Working with the game's Flash UI

The menus are Scaleform (GFx) movies driven by UnrealScript controllers (`H7MainMenuCntl`, `H7MapSelectCntl`,
...). Lua reaches them through the controllers' `GFxObject` fields and `GetObject`, `SetString`, `Invoke`,
`AttachMovie`. Lessons from building the Mods menu (`dist/mods/ModManager`):

- **Wrappers are garbage collected.** The `GFxObject` returned by `GetObject`/`AttachMovie` is a UObject that the
  engine frees when no UnrealScript variable holds it; the Flash clip stays. Look clips up by name each time.
- **`GetObject` reaches display objects and Unreal-created objects only.** Members holding arrays or objects that
  ActionScript created (list rows, dictionaries, static class members) come back `nil`.
- **Clicks**: a button added with `AttachMovie` can call a controller function through
  `AddEventListener("CLIK_click", {object = cntl, func = "Options"})`, but the event data arrives empty; hook the
  function and check `mouseX`/`mouseY` against your button.
- **Reusing a game window** is often better than building one: fill it through the same calls its controller
  makes (`SetObject("mPolledMaps", ...)`, `Invoke("AddMaps")`) and hook the controller functions its buttons call.
  Remember every value you change and put it back when the window closes, since the game reuses the window.
- **Translations are cached per movie for the whole session** (`Loca.mLocaDictionary`, filled through
  `H7FlashMovieCntl.FlashLocalize`). Overriding a key changes it everywhere; values in ALL CAPS are treated as
  keys and shown as `[VALUE]` when unknown.
- `H7Label.htmlText` resets the label's font size; prefer `text`.

## Extending the API

1. Implement the function in `src/lua/bindings.cpp` (object and engine access) or `src/lua/runtime.cpp` (mods,
   events, timers) and add it to the registration table at the end of the file (`{"name", H_name}`).
   Object helper methods (`obj:get_name()` style) go in the `H7ObjectMethods` table; they are snake_case so they
   never collide with UnrealScript members.
2. Read engine memory with `mem::Read`/`mem::ReadOr` (SEH-guarded), never raw pointers; check objects with
   `CheckValidObject`. Report misuse with `luaL_error` and a message that says what was expected.
3. Allocate anything the engine may free with `ue3::EngineRealloc`.
4. Document it in `docs/API.md` (the section and the reference table) and in the full reference under
   `docs/reference/` (signature, parameters, return values, errors), and add a check to `tests/selftest.lua`.

## Conventions

- C++20, 4-space indent, lines up to about 120 columns. Comments explain *why* (what the engine does, what was verified),
  not what the next line does. Engine facts that were measured in game say so.
- Names: `PascalCase` functions and types, `g_` globals, `k` constants; Lua API names are lowercase
  (`h7.find_all`, `obj:get_name()`), UnrealScript names keep their own spelling.
- Lua (mods and tests): 4-space indent, `local` everything, one `h7.log` prefix per mod or test.
- Keep `docs/API.md` and `docs/reference/` in step with the API, and `RESUME.md` with findings that cost time to learn.
- Commits: one topic each, imperative subject line, body explaining what and why. Work on a branch and open a
  pull request into `main`; CI must be green.
- Line endings: `.bat` files are CRLF and `.sh` files LF (`.gitattributes`); cmd and bash each break on the other.

## CI

`.github/workflows/build.yml` runs on pushes to `main` and on pull requests: it checks out with submodules on
`windows-latest`, runs `build.bat`, and uploads an `H7ModEngine` artifact laid out like the game folder:

```
Binaries/Win64/dinput8.dll
H7ModEngine/            (dist/ + API.md)
```

That artifact is what players install. The version string is `kVersion` in `src/lua/runtime.cpp`.

`.github/workflows/docs.yml` publishes the documentation site (<https://whaileee.github.io/H7ModEngine/>) to
GitHub Pages with [MkDocs Material](https://squidfunk.github.io/mkdocs-material/). The site is built from the
`docs/` folder **of a release tag**, so it always documents a released version:

- Publishing a release (not a prerelease) builds the docs at its tag and deploys them.
- *Run workflow* on the Docs workflow rebuilds them for a tag you enter (default: the latest release).
- Pull requests that change `docs/`, `mkdocs.yml` or `tools/docs/` build the site with `--strict`, so broken
  links and anchors fail the check.

The navigation is in `mkdocs.yml`; add new pages there. `tools/docs/hooks.py` puts the release tag in the site
title and turns links that leave `docs/` (such as `../src/lua/bindings.cpp`) into GitHub links at that tag.
To preview locally:

```bash
pip install -r tools/docs/requirements.txt
mkdocs serve
```

One-time repository setup: *Settings → Pages → Source: GitHub Actions*, and in *Settings → Environments →
github-pages*, allow deployments from tags (add a rule for `v*`), since release runs deploy from the tag.

## Troubleshooting

| Symptom | Likely cause |
|---|---|
| No `logs\engine.log` at all | `dinput8.dll` not in `Binaries\Win64\`, or another `dinput8.dll` there (e.g. a dangling link) |
| Log stops at `Timed out waiting for ...` | A different game version, or the game didn't finish loading |
| `Engine layout calibration failed` | Unknown build: send `dumps\probe.txt` |
| `Init complete` but no `Lua ... ready` | The tick hook doesn't fire (window minimized or in the background) |
| Nothing happens for a while, then it all runs | The game was in the background and paused its loop |
| `Unknown code token` crash in a hooked function | The trampoline misread the function's optional-parameter entries: report the function and its signature |
| `engine object ... is no longer valid` | A cached Flash `GFxObject` was garbage collected: look it up again |
| Mod changes don't take effect | You edited `dist\` but not the game copy (or ran `deploy`, which overwrote the game copy) |

```

`docs/index.md`:

```md
# H7ModEngine

A Lua 5.4 modding engine for **Might & Magic Heroes VII** (v2.2.1, 64-bit).

Mods are plain Lua files. They can read and change any game object, call any UnrealScript function, and hook game
functions to watch, change or block what they do. Mods reload automatically while the game runs, and players turn
them on and off from a **Mods** entry in the main menu.

```lua
-- mods\DailyGold\main.lua: the human player gets 500 gold every new day
h7.hook_post("MMH7Game.H7Calendar.NextDay", function(calendar)
    local controller = h7.first("H7AdventureController")
    local player = controller and controller:GetLocalPlayer(true)
    if not player then return end
    player:GetResourceSet():ModifyCurrency(500, true)
    h7.log("+500 gold for " .. player:GetName())
end)
```

## Where to start

| | |
|---|---|
| **[Modding guide](API.md)** | Installation, your first mod, the manifest, finding what to change, hooks, events, saved data. Read this first. |
| **[API reference](reference/index.md)** | Every function, method, event and value of the Lua API, with parameters, return values and errors. |
| **[Engine development](DEVELOPMENT.md)** | Building the engine, its architecture, testing in game and extending the API. |

This site is built from the documentation in the release it names (top left). The documentation for the code
on `main` lives in the [repository](https://github.com/Whaileee/H7ModEngine/tree/main/docs).

## Getting the engine

Download the latest release from [GitHub](https://github.com/Whaileee/H7ModEngine/releases) and follow
[Installation](API.md#installation).

H7ModEngine is an unofficial fan project. It is not affiliated with or endorsed by Ubisoft. Might & Magic and
Heroes are trademarks of Ubisoft Entertainment. Use mods at your own risk and keep backups of your saves.

`docs/reference/events.md`:

```md
# Events and timers

Events and timers run on the game thread, once per rendered frame. **They pause while the game window is in the
background**, because the game stops updating then.

Each frame the engine, in this order: checks for changed mod files and map changes and runs the `exec` folder
(about once a second), applies mods turned on or off, reloads mods whose `reload` setting changed, handles
[`h7.reload()`](h7.md#h7reload), runs due timers, and fires [`tick`](#tick).

## Events

Handle an event with [`h7.on(event, fn)`](h7.md#h7on); `fn` receives the arguments below. Remove the handler
with [`h7.off(id)`](h7.md#h7off). Handlers run in the order they were added; an error in one is logged and the
others still run.

### tick

```lua
h7.on("tick", function(dt) end)
```

| Argument | |
|---|---|
| `dt` | Seconds since the previous frame (number) |

Fires every frame, after timers. Keep handlers cheap; for anything periodic use [`h7.every`](h7.md#h7every).

### map_loaded

```lua
h7.on("map_loaded", function(map) end)
```

| Argument | |
|---|---|
| `map` | Name of the map now active, lowercase: `h7logoentry` (intro), `councilhub` (main menu), `sce_sephinroth`, ... |

Fires when a different map becomes active (checked about once a second), including the first map after startup.
Battles don't change the map and don't fire it. Objects of the previous map are gone: drop references and caches
you kept.

### mods_loaded

```lua
h7.on("mods_loaded", function(count) end)
```

| Argument | |
|---|---|
| `count` | Number of mods loaded |

Fires after every enabled mod has loaded, at startup and after [`h7.reload()`](h7.md#h7reload). Use it for work
that needs other mods to be ready.

### mod_toggled

```lua
h7.on("mod_toggled", function(name, enabled) end)
```

| Argument | |
|---|---|
| `name` | The mod's name |
| `enabled` | `true` if it was loaded, `false` if it was unloaded |

Fires after a mod was loaded or unloaded because of [`h7.set_mod_enabled`](h7.md#h7set_mod_enabled) (the Mods
menu). A mod that was just unloaded doesn't receive it.

### setting_changed

```lua
h7.on("setting_changed", function(name, key, value) end)
```

| Argument | |
|---|---|
| `name` | The mod whose setting changed |
| `key` | The setting's key |
| `value` | The new value |

Fires when a [setting](mod.md#settings) gets a different value: from the Mods menu,
[`mod.settings.key = v`](mod.md#modsettings) or [`h7.set_mod_setting`](h7.md#h7set_mod_setting). It fires for
every mod's settings, so check `name`:

```lua
h7.on("setting_changed", function(name, key, value)
    if name == mod.name and key == "gold" then h7.log("Gold per day is now " .. value) end
end)
```

## Timers

| Function | |
|---|---|
| [`h7.after(seconds, fn)`](h7.md#h7after) | Run `fn()` once after `seconds` |
| [`h7.every(seconds, fn)`](h7.md#h7every) | Run `fn()` every `seconds` |
| [`h7.cancel(id)`](h7.md#h7cancel) | Cancel a timer |

Timers count **game time**: the sum of frame times since the engine started, which stops while the window is in
the background. A timer runs on the first frame at or after its due time, so its precision is one frame.

A repeating timer is rescheduled `seconds` after the frame it ran on: it doesn't catch up on missed runs and runs
at most once per frame. `fn` receives no arguments. Errors in it are logged and a repeating timer keeps running.

Timers are removed when the mod that created them unloads.

```

`docs/reference/h7.md`:

```md
# h7 library

The global `h7` table holds the engine's API. It is shared by every mod and by `exec` scripts.

Parameters called **class** take a class name (`"H7AdventureHero"` or `"MMH7Game.H7AdventureHero"`) or a class
object; **func** takes a function path (`"MMH7Game.H7Calendar.NextDay"`, optionally prefixed with `"Function "`)
or a function object.

## Engine

### h7.version

```lua
h7.version → string
```

The engine version, e.g. `"0.1.0"`. Use it to check for features your mod needs.

## Logging

All logging goes to `<Game>\H7ModEngine\logs\engine.log` (the previous run is kept in `engine.prev.log`). Each
line starts with a timestamp and the name of the mod that wrote it, or `[lua]` for code that runs outside any mod
(`exec` scripts).

### h7.log

```lua
h7.log(...)
print(...)
```

Writes its arguments, converted with `tostring` and separated by tabs, as one line:

```lua
h7.log("Hero", hero, "has", 12, "movement points")
-- [19:32:14.962] [MyMod] Hero	H7AdventureHero sce_sephinroth.TheWorld.PersistentLevel.H7AdventureHero_0	has	12	movement points
```

`print` is the same function.

### h7.warn

```lua
h7.warn(...)
```

Like [`h7.log`](#h7log), with the line prefixed `WARNING`.

### h7.error

```lua
h7.error(...)
```

Like [`h7.log`](#h7log), with the line prefixed `ERROR`. It only **logs**; it does not raise an error or stop
your code. To stop with an error, use Lua's `error("message")`.

## Finding objects

### h7.first

```lua
h7.first(class) → object | nil
```

The first live instance of `class` or one of its subclasses, or `nil` if there is none. Class default objects
(`Default__...`) and archetypes (templates stored in content packages) are skipped.

| Parameter | |
|---|---|
| `class` | Class name or class object |

**Errors:** `unknown class '<name>'` if a class name doesn't exist.

```lua
local controller = h7.first("H7AdventureController")
if controller then h7.log(controller:GetLocalPlayer(true):GetName()) end
```

"First" means lowest in the engine's object table, which usually but not always is the one created first. When
there can be several instances (heroes, players, armies), use [`h7.find_all`](#h7find_all) and pick the one you
want.

### h7.find_all

```lua
h7.find_all(class [, include_templates]) → { object, ... }
```

Every live instance of `class` or its subclasses, as a sequence (empty when there are none).

| Parameter | |
|---|---|
| `class` | Class name or class object |
| `include_templates` | `true` to include class default objects and archetypes. Default `false` |

**Errors:** `unknown class '<name>'`.

```lua
for _, hero in ipairs(h7.find_all("H7AdventureHero")) do
    h7.log(hero:GetName(), hero:GetLevel())
end

-- every creature archetype the game has loaded
local creatures = h7.find_all("H7Creature", true)
```

`find_all` scans the whole engine object table. Call it when you need it, not every frame,
and cache the result while it stays valid (until the next `map_loaded`, for objects in a map).

### h7.find

```lua
h7.find(path) → object | nil
```

The object with the given path name, or `nil`. The path may be prefixed with the object's class to tell apart
objects with the same path.

| Parameter | |
|---|---|
| `path` | `"Package.Outer.Name"`, or `"ClassName Package.Outer.Name"` |

```lua
local calendar_class = h7.find("Class MMH7Game.H7Calendar")
local next_day = h7.find("Function MMH7Game.H7Calendar.NextDay")
local pixie = h7.find("Creature_Pixie.A_CreaturePixie")   -- an archetype in a content package
```

`dumps\objects.txt` (written at startup) lists every object's path.

### h7.find_class

```lua
h7.find_class(name) → class | nil
```

The class called `name` (`"Package.Class"` or the bare `"Class"`), or `nil`.

```lua
local hero_class = h7.find_class("H7AdventureHero")
h7.log(hero_class:get_super())   -- Class MMH7Game.H7EditorHero
```

### h7.enum

```lua
h7.enum(name) → { [string] = integer }
```

A table mapping each name of the UnrealScript enum `name` to its numeric value (`0`, `1`, ...). Enum values read
from properties and function results are plain integers; use this table to compare them or pass them.

| Parameter | |
|---|---|
| `name` | The enum's name (`"EUnitType"`) or path (`"MMH7Game.AH7StructsAndEnumsNative.EUnitType"`) |

**Errors:** `unknown enum '<name>'`.

```lua
local UnitType = h7.enum("EUnitType")
if unit:GetEntityType() == UnitType.UNIT_HERO then ... end
```

## Reflection

### h7.properties

```lua
h7.properties(x) → { { name, type, owner, offset }, ... }
```

Every property of a class or struct, inherited ones included.

| Parameter | |
|---|---|
| `x` | A class or struct name; an object (lists its class); a class, struct or function object; or a struct reference |

| Field | |
|---|---|
| `name` | Property name, as used from Lua |
| `type` | The engine's property type, e.g. `IntProperty`, `ObjectProperty`, `ArrayProperty`, `StructProperty` |
| `owner` | The class or struct that declares it |
| `offset` | Byte offset inside the object or struct |

Passing a function object lists its parameters and local variables. Properties of types the engine doesn't
support are left out.

**Errors:** `unknown class/struct '<name>'`.

```lua
for _, p in ipairs(h7.properties("MMH7Game.H7Calendar")) do h7.log(p.owner, p.type, p.name) end
```

### h7.functions

```lua
h7.functions(x) → { { name, type, owner, flags }, ... }
```

Every function of a class (or struct), inherited ones included. `x` takes the same values as in
[`h7.properties`](#h7properties).

| Field | |
|---|---|
| `name` | Function name |
| `type` | The function object's class (`"Function"`) |
| `owner` | The class that declares it. A function overridden in a subclass appears once per declaring class |
| `flags` | The engine's function flags (bit field: `0x400` native, `0x800` event, `0x2000` static, ...) |

**Errors:** `unknown class/struct '<name>'`.

### h7.signature

```lua
h7.signature(func) → string
```

The function's declaration in UnrealScript syntax: `static` / `native` / `event` qualifiers, return type, full
path and parameters with `out` and `optional`.

**Errors:** `function '<path>' not found (use Package.Class.Function)`; `expected a Function object`.

```lua
h7.log(h7.signature("MMH7Game.H7GameProcessor.CalculateDamage"))
-- function void MMH7Game.H7GameProcessor.CalculateDamage(out H7CombatResult Result, int resultIdx, bool forecast)
```

Check the signature before hooking a function: `native` functions can be hooked but give the hook no arguments
and can't be blocked (see [h7.hook](#h7hook)).

### h7.to_table

```lua
h7.to_table(value) → table | value
```

A plain Lua copy of an engine value, independent of the engine:

| `value` | Result |
|---|---|
| Struct reference | Table of its fields |
| Array reference | Sequence of its elements |
| Object reference | Table of the object's properties |
| Anything else | `value` itself |

Structs and arrays inside are copied recursively; objects inside stay object references (they are not expanded,
since object graphs loop). Use it to log a value, or to keep hook arguments after the hook returns.

```lua
h7.hook_post("MMH7Game.H7GameProcessor.CalculateDamage", function(proc, args)
    last_result = h7.to_table(args.Result)    -- args.Result itself is invalid after the hook
end)
```

## Hooks

A hook runs a Lua function whenever a game function is called, from UnrealScript or from the engine's C++ code.
See [Hooks](../API.md#hooks) in the guide for what hooks see and how to keep them fast.

### h7.hook

```lua
h7.hook(func, fn [, when]) → id
```

Adds a hook on `func` and returns its id (an integer, for [`h7.unhook`](#h7unhook)).

| Parameter | |
|---|---|
| `func` | Function path or function object |
| `fn` | The hook function, see below |
| `when` | `"pre"` (default): before the function runs; `"post"`: after it, like [`h7.hook_post`](#h7hook_post) |

A pre-hook is called as `fn(self, args)`:

| Argument | |
|---|---|
| `self` | The object the function was called on |
| `args` | The function's parameters by name, readable and writable (a [struct reference](objects.md#hook-arguments)). `nil` for native functions |

Return `true` (the boolean) to **block** the call: the original function doesn't run and its caller gets a zero
return value (`0`, `false`, `""`, `None`). Post-hooks still run. Any other return value lets the call proceed.
Changing `args` changes what the function receives.

**Errors:** the function isn't found; `could not hook <path>`.

```lua
-- No hero may visit obelisks
h7.hook("MMH7Game.H7Obelisk.OnVisit", function(obelisk, args)
    return true
end)
```

Errors inside `fn` are logged with a stack trace and the call continues as if the hook had returned nothing;
the hook stays installed.

Hooks on **native** functions (`native` in [`h7.signature`](#h7signature)) receive `args = nil` and can't
block; the engine logs a note when you add a pre-hook to one. Latent and iterator functions only report calls
made from C++.

### h7.hook_post

```lua
h7.hook_post(func, fn) → id
```

Adds a hook that runs after `func` has run. Same as `h7.hook(func, fn, "post")`. `fn` is called as
`fn(self, args, result)`:

| Argument | |
|---|---|
| `self` | The object the function was called on |
| `args` | The parameters after the call, `out` parameters included (`nil` for native functions) |
| `result` | The return value. Not passed for functions that return nothing |

Return a non-`nil` value to **replace the return value**; return nothing to leave it. A replacement of the wrong
type is logged as an error and the original value is kept.

```lua
h7.hook_post("MMH7Game.H7CombatArmy.GetKilledCreaturesXP", function(army, args, xp)
    if xp > 0 then return math.floor(xp * 1.5 + 0.5) end
end)
```

Several hooks on one function all run in the order they were added: every pre-hook, then (unless blocked) the
function, then every post-hook. When two post-hooks replace the result, the later one wins and receives the
earlier one's value as `result`.

### h7.unhook

```lua
h7.unhook(id)
```

Removes the hook `id`. Unknown ids are ignored. Hooks are removed automatically when their mod unloads.

## Events and timers

See [Events and timers](events.md) for the list of events and how timers are scheduled.

### h7.on

```lua
h7.on(event, fn) → id
```

Calls `fn(...)` every time `event` fires, with the [event's arguments](events.md#events). Returns the handler's
id. Handlers run in the order they were added. Any event name is accepted, so a misspelt name simply never fires.

```lua
h7.on("map_loaded", function(map) h7.log("Entered " .. map) end)
```

### h7.off

```lua
h7.off(id)
```

Removes the event handler `id`. Unknown ids are ignored.

### h7.after

```lua
h7.after(seconds, fn) → id
```

Calls `fn()` once, `seconds` of game time from now (on the first frame at or after that time). Returns the
timer's id.

```lua
h7.after(5, function() h7.log("5 seconds later") end)
```

### h7.every

```lua
h7.every(seconds, fn) → id
```

Calls `fn()` every `seconds` of game time until cancelled. The first call is `seconds` from now. At most one call
per frame: a timer shorter than a frame runs once per frame. `seconds` of `0` or less makes a one-shot timer,
like [`h7.after`](#h7after).

### h7.cancel

```lua
h7.cancel(id)
```

Cancels the timer `id`. Unknown ids (including timers that already fired) are ignored. A timer may cancel
itself.

```lua
local n, id = 0, nil
id = h7.every(1, function()
    n = n + 1
    if n == 10 then h7.cancel(id) end
end)
```

## Creating objects

See [Creating objects](../API.md#creating-objects) in the guide for how copies interact with the garbage
collector, lazily loaded references, localisation and save games.

### h7.clone

```lua
h7.clone(source, name [, outer [, flags]]) → object, created
```

Creates a copy of `source`: an object of the same class with every property copied from it.

| Parameter | |
|---|---|
| `source` | The object to copy, usually an archetype (a template such as `Creature_Pixie.A_CreaturePixie`) |
| `name` | Name of the new object |
| `outer` | The object or package that contains it. Default: the transient package. Use [`h7.package`](#h7package) for stable paths |
| `flags` | Extra [object flags](#h7flags) to add, e.g. `h7.flags.RootSet` |

| Returns | |
|---|---|
| `object` | The copy |
| `created` | `true` if it was created now; `false` if `outer` already held an object called `name` of the same class, which is returned instead |

The copy keeps the source's `Public`, `Standalone` and `Archetype` flags, so a copy of an archetype is an
archetype too. Returning an existing copy makes mod code safe to run again after a hot reload.

**Errors:** `<path> already exists with another class`; `could not create <class> '<name>': <reason>` (for
example when [`h7.can_create()`](#h7can_create) is `false`).

```lua
local pkg = h7.package("MyMod")
local sprite = h7.clone(h7.find("Creature_Pixie.A_CreaturePixie"), "A_CreatureFrostSprite", pkg, h7.flags.RootSet)
sprite.mHitpoints = 20
```

### h7.new_object

```lua
h7.new_object(class [, options]) → object
```

Creates a new object of `class`. `options` is a table with any of:

| Field | |
|---|---|
| `name` | Object name. Default: a generated unique name |
| `outer` | Containing object or package. Default: the transient package |
| `template` | Object to copy property values from. Default: the class defaults |
| `flags` | [Object flags](#h7flags) (replaces the default `Public + Standalone`) |
| `archetype` | `true` to add the `Archetype` flag |
| `replace` | `true` to reconstruct an existing object with the same name in place (same address, properties reset) instead of failing |

**Errors:** `unknown class '<name>'`; `<path> already exists (pass replace = true to reconstruct it)`;
`could not create <class> '<name>': <reason>`.

```lua
local copy = h7.new_object("H7Creature", { name = "A_MyCreature", outer = h7.package("MyMod"),
                                           template = h7.find("Creature_Pixie.A_CreaturePixie"), archetype = true })
```

### h7.package

```lua
h7.package(name) → package
```

The top-level package called `name`, created if it doesn't exist. Objects inside it have stable paths
(`MyMod.A_CreatureFrostSprite`), which is what save games store.

**Errors:** `could not create package '<name>': <reason>`.

### h7.name

```lua
h7.name(str) → str
```

Adds `str` to the engine's name table (if it isn't there yet) and returns it. Writing a `name` property does this
for you; call it yourself when a name must exist before the game looks it up.

**Errors:** `could not create name '<str>'`.

### h7.can_create

```lua
h7.can_create() → boolean
```

`true` when the engine found and verified the game functions object creation needs. When `false`, the reason is
in the log and [`h7.clone`](#h7clone), [`h7.new_object`](#h7new_object), [`h7.package`](#h7package) (for a new
package) and [`h7.name`](#h7name) (for a new name) fail.

### h7.flags

```lua
h7.flags.Public | .Standalone | .Transient | .Archetype | .RootSet → integer
```

Object flags for [`h7.clone`](#h7clone) and [`h7.new_object`](#h7new_object). Combine them with `|`.

| Flag | Value | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `Public` | `0x0000000400000000` | Visible outside its package |
| `Standalone` | `0x0008000000000000` | Kept while its package is loaded, even when unreferenced |
| `Transient` | `0x0000400000000000` | Never saved |
| `Archetype` | `0x0000000000000400` | A template other objects are created from |
| `RootSet` | `0x0000000000004000` | Never garbage collected |

## Mods

### h7.mods

```lua
h7.mods() → { { name, version, author, description, category, enabled, loaded, hidden, settings }, ... }
```

Every installed mod, in load order.

| Field | |
|---|---|
| `name`, `version`, `author`, `description`, `category` | From the [manifest](mod.md#manifest) (empty strings when not set) |
| `enabled` | Whether the mod is turned on (manifest `enabled`, overridden by the player's choice in `H7ModEngine.ini`) |
| `loaded` | Whether it is loaded. `enabled` but not `loaded`: it has no `main.lua`, or is about to load on the next frame. A mod whose `main.lua` raised an error still counts as loaded (see the log) |
| `hidden` | Manifest `hidden`: not listed in the Mods menu |
| `settings` | How many [settings](mod.md#settings) it declares |

### h7.set_mod_enabled

```lua
h7.set_mod_enabled(name, on)
```

Turns the mod `name` on (`on` truthy) or off. The choice is saved in `H7ModEngine.ini` and the mod loads or
unloads at the start of the next frame, after which [`mod_toggled`](events.md#mod_toggled) fires. A mod may
disable itself.

**Errors:** `unknown mod '<name>'`.

### h7.mod_settings

```lua
h7.mod_settings(name) → { setting, ... }
```

The settings the mod `name` declares, in manifest order, each with its current value:

| Field | |
|---|---|
| `key`, `label`, `description` | As declared (`label` defaults to `key`) |
| `type` | `"bool"`, `"number"`, `"choice"` or `"text"` |
| `value` | Current value |
| `default` | Declared default |
| `min`, `max`, `step` | Numbers only, when declared |
| `options` | Choices only: the allowed values |
| `reload` | Whether changing it restarts the mod |
| `control` | Numbers only, when declared: `"slider"`, `"input"` or `"arrows"` |

**Errors:** `unknown mod '<name>'`.

### h7.set_mod_setting

```lua
h7.set_mod_setting(name, key, value) → stored value
```

Changes a setting of mod `name`, exactly like the Mods menu does: the value is validated (numbers are clamped
to `min`..`max` and snapped to `step`), saved in `H7ModEngine.ini`, and
[`setting_changed`](events.md#setting_changed) fires if it changed. Returns the value as stored. A mod changes
its own settings with [`mod.settings.key = value`](mod.md#modsettings).

**Errors:** `unknown mod '<name>'`; `mod <name> has no setting '<key>'`; `invalid value for <name>.<key> (<type>)`
(e.g. a string for a number, or a value not in `options`).

### h7.reload

```lua
h7.reload()
```

At the start of the next frame, unloads every mod, scans the `mods` folder again (picking up new mod folders) and
loads every enabled mod; then [`mods_loaded`](events.md#mods_loaded) fires.

## Console

### h7.exec

```lua
h7.exec(command) → string
```

Runs an Unreal console command through the local player controller (`PlayerController.ConsoleCommand`) and
returns its output. Which commands work depends on the game's (shipping) build.

**Errors:** `no PlayerController exists yet` (very early during startup).

```

`docs/reference/index.md`:

```md
# API reference

The complete Lua API of H7ModEngine. For an introduction with worked examples, read the
[modding guide](../API.md) first.

Every mod runs in a Lua 5.4 state shared by all mods, with the standard libraries (`string`, `table`, `math`,
`io`, `os`, `utf8`, `coroutine`, `debug`, `package`) and these additions:

| Name | What it is | Reference |
|---|---|---|
| `h7` | The engine library: finding objects, hooks, events, timers, mods, creating objects, logging | [h7 library](h7.md) |
| `mod` | The current mod: its name, folder, settings, saved data and `require` | [Mods](mod.md#the-mod-table) |
| `print(...)` | Same as `h7.log(...)` | [h7.log](h7.md#h7log) |
| `require(name)` | Standard `require`, searching `<Game>\H7ModEngine\lib\?.lua` | [Mods](mod.md#shared-libraries) |
| `on_unload()` | A global function a mod may define; called before the mod unloads | [Mods](mod.md#on_unload) |

Values coming from the engine are one of three reference types, plus plain Lua values:

| Type | What it is | Reference |
|---|---|---|
| Object reference | A live engine object (actor, hero, class, function, ...) | [Engine objects](objects.md#object-references) |
| Struct reference | A live view of a struct inside an object, a struct or hook arguments | [Structs](objects.md#struct-references) |
| Array reference | A live view of an UnrealScript `array<T>` | [Arrays](objects.md#array-references) |

How every UnrealScript type is converted is in [Values and types](types.md).
